## Document 013: 12月加息或成衰退导火索？对冲大佬阿克曼呼吁美联储将2%通胀红线上移至3%

Source PDF: all report pdf/12月加息或成衰退导火索？对冲大佬阿克曼呼吁美联储将2%通胀红线上移至3%.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⽐尔 · 阿克曼警告，美联储若在 12 ⽉继续加息，可能加剧⾦融条件收紧，将美国乃⾄全球经
济推⼊衰退。 SpaceX 融资成本攀升、股市下跌均显⽰融资环境趋紧。阿克曼同时主张将通
胀⽬标上调⾄ 3% ，认为地缘⻛险、关税及 AI 投资热潮已改变全球经济结构。
摘要：
美联储是否应该继续加息，正⾯临越来越⼤的争议。对冲基⾦⼤佬⽐尔 · 阿克曼（Bill Ackman ）
警告，当前⾦融条件已⼤幅收紧，如果美联储在 12 ⽉ 9 ⽇的政策会议上继续加息，将是严重的政
策失误，甚⾄可能把美国乃⾄全球经济推⼊衰退。
11 ⽉ 23 ⽇，阿克曼在 X 平台发⽂称，美联储可能在 12 ⽉ 9 ⽇ “ 再犯⼀个错误 ” ，因为⾦融条件已经
明显收紧。他以 SpaceX 为例指出，该公司近期有担保债务融资利率达到 8.259% ，⽽此前发⾏⽆
担保债券时的利率尚不⾜ 6% 。与此同时，近期股市⼤幅下跌也进⼀步推⾼了企业股权融资成
本。
除了反对进⼀步加息，阿克曼还提出了⼀项更具争议性的主张：将美联储⻓期通胀⽬标从 2% 上

调⾄ 3% 。他认为，地缘⻛险、全球国防开⽀上升、关税壁垒，以及⼈⼯智能基础设施的⼤规模
投资，正在改变全球经济的运⾏环境。
在他看来，继续坚持 2% 的通胀⽬标，可能迫使美联储采取过度紧缩的政策，以牺牲经济增⻓为
代价换取更低的通胀。
⾦融条件明显收紧， SpaceX 融资成本攀升
据此前报道， SpaceX 安排了约 440 亿美元的债务融资⽅案，⽤于采购英伟达芯⽚及相关设备，
以满⾜⼈⼯智能基础设施建设需求。
阿克曼指出， SpaceX 近期有担保债务融资利率达到 8.259% ，明显⾼于此前⽆担保债券不⾜ 6%
的融资利率。与此同时，股市下跌也抬⾼了企业股权融资成本。在他看来，债务和股权融资渠
道同时承压，反映出⾦融条件已经显著收紧。
美联储 10 ⽉ 27 ⽇⾄ 28 ⽇ FOMC 会议纪要也显⽰，多数与会者注意到，⾃ 9 ⽉会议以来⾦融条件有
所收紧，表现为抵押贷款利率上升，以及⾼⻛险借款⼈和⼈⼯智能相关借款⼈的信⽤利差⾛
阔。
不过，通胀仍是美联储⾯临的政策难题。⼯作⼈员估计， 9 ⽉整体 PCE 通胀率为 3.6% ，核⼼

PCE 通胀率为 3.1% ，均明显⾼于 2% 的⽬标。
美联储 10 ⽉将联邦基⾦利率维持在 3.75% ⾄ 4% 不变，但有两名委员⽀持加息 25 个基点。会议纪
要显⽰，⼏乎所有与会者都认为，在基准情景下进⼀步加息 “ 可能是适当的 ” 。下⼀次政策会议定

于 12 ⽉ 8 ⽇⾄ 9 ⽇举⾏。
阿克曼主张将通胀⽬标从 2% 上调⾄ 3%
阿克曼认为， 2% 的通胀⽬标已难以适应当前经济环境。地缘政治紧张局势推动国防开⽀增加，
关税壁垒抬⾼供应链成本，⼈⼯智能基础设施及能源领域的⼤规模投资也带来新的资本需求。
这些结构性变化使维持低通胀⾯临更⼤挑战。
他认为，美联储不应为了将通胀压回 2% ，⽽不惜牺牲经济增⻓。相较之下， 3% 的通胀⽬标是新
经济环境下更现实的选择。
美联储⽬前仍将 PCE 价格指数年度涨幅 2% 作为⻓期通胀⽬标。

## Document 031: 20261015【投研報告】市場快訊_通膨升溫壓抑美股表現，近期聚焦財報結果

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261015【投研報告】市場快訊_通膨升溫壓抑美股表現，近期聚焦財報結果.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (市場評論)
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決

大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
通膨升溫壓抑美股表現，近期聚焦財報結果
2026 年 10 月 15 日
一、 市場動態
1. 科技股領跌，費半連 6 日收黑：美股主要指數 10 月 14 日全數下跌，道瓊工業指數下
跌 0.09%，收 51,621.48 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.11%，收 7,718.94 點，為 7 月 29
日以來最大單日跌幅；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 1.88%，收 26,692.87 點，為 7 月 23
日以來最大單日跌幅。費城半導體指數下跌 4.30%，收 11,684.38 點，連續第６個交
易日走低，為 2025 年 5 月以來最長連跌紀錄。

2. 類股非全面下挫，資金仍呈輪動現象：半導體及 AI 相關股票在 10/14 跌幅尤其明顯
，AMD 下跌 5.73%、Nvidia 下跌 3.16%、Broadcom 下跌 3.10%，AI 基礎建設相關
的 CoreWeave、Nebius、Oracle 及 Dell 跌幅更深，顯示市場對資本支出規模較大、
融資成本敏感度較高的 AI 相關企業風險偏好明顯下降。不過，S&P 500 的 11 大類
股中有 8 類下跌，資訊科技及通訊服務為主要拖累來源，但醫療保健、必需消費與能
源類股仍逆勢上漲。
3. 美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中急升後回落：美國 10 年期公債殖利率 10/14 由 5.2%左
右快速升至約 5.33%，逼近近期高點；然而，隨後市場消化 Fed 官員較審慎的政策訊
號，殖利率自高檔回落，終場約收於 5.19%，較前一交易日 5.21%小幅下降約 2 個基
點。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. 美 9 月通膨高於預期，Fed 再升息風險重新升高：美國 10/14 公布 9 月 CPI 月增 0.6%

、年增率也由 8 月的 3.4%上揚至 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%、年增率由 8 月的 2.4%
升至 2.6%。其中汽油價格單月大漲 9.1%，貢獻整體 CPI 月增幅超過一半；住宅成本
亦月增 0.4%，顯示物價壓力仍具黏性。此數據公布後，市場重新提高 Fed 進一步升
息的預期，CME FedWatch 所反映的 10 月升息 1 碼機率一度由前一日約 22%提高至
37%。雖然截至 10/15 台北時間早上 7 點左右該機率回降至 29.3%，這也提醒投資人
Fed 的升息路徑仍存在不確定性。
2. 高利率同時壓縮科技股估值與提高 AI 融資成本：科技及半導體公司未來獲利成長占評
價的比重較高，因此對折現率變化相對敏感。當長天期美債殖利率維持 5%以上，市
場願意支付的本益比自然受到限制，即使企業獲利仍維持成長，股價也可能因評價倍
數下降而出現修正。另一方面，本輪 AI 投資需要大量資料中心、伺服器、電力與網路
基礎設施，高利率亦直接增加企業融資成本，反映信用市場對高度依賴外部資金的 AI

基礎建設公司要求更高風險溢酬。

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. AI 投資焦點由「支出規模」轉向「投資報酬率」:近期市場對 AI 的疑慮逐漸由「需求
是否成長」，轉向「龐大的資本支出能否轉化為足夠營收、獲利與自由現金流」。
Broadcom 執行長陳福陽近期亦指出，AI 未來發展的重要限制可能逐漸由晶片或記憶
體供應轉向資本本身。我們認為，這並不代表 AI 資本支出週期即將結束，而是市場已
由第一階段的「資本支出愈大、成長想像愈高」，逐步進入更重視資本效率與投資報酬
率的第二階段。未來財務結構、現金流與 AI 商業化能力，將成為不同企業評價分化的
重要因素。
4. 中國記憶體競爭疑慮加重半導體短線壓力：中國長鑫存儲（CXMT）10/10 傳出記憶

體技術取得進展，引發市場對未來 DRAM 供給增加及價格競爭的疑慮，記憶體相關類
股率先承壓，並進一步影響整體半導體市場情緒。惟 DRAM 與 GPU、客製化 ASIC、
先進晶圓製程及先進封裝的產業競爭與需求結構並不相同。我們認為，儘管記憶體競
爭升溫值得關注，但目前尚不足以延伸為整體 AI 半導體需求同步轉弱。
5. 高檔籌碼加速獲利了結：AI 與半導體仍是 2026 年美股表現最強勢的投資主題之一，
部分科技股今年累計漲幅已相當可觀。當通膨、殖利率及 AI 投資報酬疑慮同時升高，
先前漲幅較大、投資人部位較集中的族群自然成為優先調節對象。因此，本波費半快
速修正除了總經與基本面疑慮外，亦包含籌碼降溫及風險部位收縮因素，使短期價格
波動幅度可能高於企業基本面實際變化。
三、市場看法
1. 類股輪動明顯，尚非全面看空美股：科技與 AI 相關股票 10 月 14 日明顯承壓，但醫
療保健、必需消費與能源類股仍逆勢上漲，道瓊工業指數僅下跌 0.09%。此外，S&P
500 成分股下跌家數與上漲家數之比約為 1.1：1；相較於指數下跌 1.11%，市場廣度

並未同步大幅惡化。這反映資金主要由高評價且前期漲幅較大的科技與 AI 族群，轉向
現金流較穩定的防禦型產業。現階段市場特徵較接近類股輪動與風格切換，而非無差
別的全面風險撤離，亦尚未看到美股企業獲利或經濟基本面全面反轉的明確訊號。
2. 美股科技股財報前風險降載，Q3 仍存在重新定價空間：近期科技股修正，費城半導
體指數連續６個交易日下跌，也可能部分反映第三季財報季正式展開前，投資人在股
價先前漲幅較大、評價偏高及利率環境不確定下，提前降低風險曝險。真正決定後續
市場方向的關鍵，仍在於實際企業獲利能否支持目前的高成長預期。根據 Bloomberg
季度獲利成長追蹤資料，今年 Q1 與 Q2 財報季期間的 EPS 成長率經驗顯示，隨實際
財報陸續公布，企業獲利成長曾明顯優於財報季開始前的市場預期。儘管這並不保證
Q3 將複製前兩季的上修幅度，但目前市場因財報不確定性所反映的風險折價仍存在
被實際財報重新驗證的可能。若大型科技及半導體公司後續營收、獲利與 AI 需求指引
實際公布結果優於預期，近期風險溢價可望部分收斂；反之，若獲利成長無法跟上目

前評價，市場整理時間則可能進一步延長。

不另行通知。
3. 費半修正幅度大，但 AI 需求尚未出現全面反轉：ASML 在 10/14 所公布的最新第三
季財報反而顯示 AI 相關設備需求仍相當強勁，該公司表示 2027 與 2028 年規劃產能
可能仍無法完全滿足客戶需求，部分客戶亦已表達對 2029 及 2030 年設備需求的興
趣。儘管費城半導體指數近期市場情緒轉弱，但由 ASML 的財報表現來看，目前仍未
看到大型 AI 客戶全面取消晶片訂單、主要雲端服務商同步削減 AI 資本支出，或先進
製程需求快速降溫等結構性訊號。
4. 台積電營收顯示 AI 需求暢旺，留意 10/15 法說會內容：台積電 10/8 公布的第三季營

收達新台幣 1.49 兆元、年增約 50%，創歷史新高，並高於市場原先預估的 1.46 兆元
；9 月單月營收年增 54.6%。強勁的營收表現仍反映 AI 與先進製程需求具有支撐。目
前市場因此呈現「企業營收仍快速成長，但股票評價下降」的狀況。高殖利率環境下
，EPS 上升與本益比下降可以同時發生，後續股價方向將取決於企業獲利上修幅度是
否足以抵銷評價收縮。該公司將於 10/15 下午舉行第三季法人說明會。市場重點將集
中於第四季展望、AI 與 HPC 需求、先進製程、先進封裝及未來資本支出規劃。公司
對 AI 需求與產能投資的最新判斷，將成為判斷近期費半修正主要屬於評價調整，或產
業基本面是否開始出現變化的重要依據。
5. AI 與半導體進入基本面分化階段：我們認為，在高利率環境下，高度仰賴外部融資、
自由現金流尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者，受到的資金成本壓力明顯高於具穩定現金流
的大型科技與雲端服務商。同樣地，記憶體、GPU、ASIC、先進製程及先進封裝各有
不同供需結構。我們預期下一階段 AI 行情將更加重視訂單能見度、產品競爭力、毛利

率、自由現金流及 AI 營收實際兌現，產業內部強弱分化程度可能進一步提高。
6. Fed 路徑仍存不確定性，惟企業獲利可望提供股市支撐：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使市場重
新評估 Fed 後續升息時點與幅度，美國利率政策仍存在不確定性，長天期美債殖利率
也可能維持高檔。不過，現階段企業獲利成長與 AI 相關需求仍具韌性，若第三季財報

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
延續前兩季優於預期的趨勢，企業 EPS 若能上修有望部分抵銷高利率對本益比的壓縮
效果。換言之，後續市場表現未必只由 Fed 政策主導，企業實際獲利能否持續兌現，
將是支撐科技與半導體股中期走勢的重要力量。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦美股財報與 Fed 決策，科技股中期趨勢料不致有變：近期市場同時面臨財報
前的觀望態勢、美債殖利率偏高及 AI 資本支出疑慮等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能
受到壓抑，後續宜關注 10 月美股財報對企業獲利與資本支出展望，以及 FOMC 會議
所釋出的政策訊號。中期而言，目前 AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡

象，科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，但在利率維持高檔及市場評價偏高的環境下，後
續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出的實際變化。
2. 中天期債券風險報酬仍較均衡：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使短端殖利率持續受到 Fed 政策路
徑影響，而 20～30 年超長端仍面臨財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價偏高等結構性壓
力。相較之下，目前殖利率已位於歷史相對高檔，若後續核心通膨或能源價格改善，
中段殖利率具有回落條件，同時其存續期間風險又低於超長端，因此現階段以中天期
美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

美國就業及企業活動持續改善，期中選舉牽動財政與科技監管政策 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數由 54.5 升至 55.5，連續第 10 個
月維持於擴張區間；新訂單、生產及庫存表現改善，顯示需求與生產動
能仍具韌性。惟投入成本仍處高檔，且關稅政策、地緣政治及供應鏈風
險猶存，後續發展仍有待進一步觀察。 
⚫ 10 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數為 54.5，較上月 54.9 略降 0.4 個
百分點，但已連續 28 個月維持擴張，服務業景氣持續增長。新訂單與
商業活動雖趨緩，就業續呈擴張，未完成訂單創 2022 年 7 月以來高點；
新出口訂單重返擴張區間，顯示內外需求仍具韌性，惟關稅、能源價格
及成本壓力仍為後續觀察重點。 
⚫ 10 月美國非農就業增加 16.5 萬人，高於上月修正後之 6.0 萬人，失業
率由 4.2%降至 4.1%，加以前兩個月就業人數合計上修 8.4 萬人，顯示
勞動市場較前期有所回溫。惟新增就業仍主要集中於專業及商業服務、
醫療保健及運輸倉儲等部分服務業，製造業增幅相對有限，反映勞動市
場雖維持韌性，但部分就業品質及企業用工需求仍存在壓力。 
⚫ 9 月美國職位空缺數由 719.0 萬個增至 752.3 萬個，職缺率由 4.3%升至
4.5%，企業徵才需求有所回升；聘僱人數亦同步增加，聘僱率由 3.3%
升至 3.4%。整體而言，企業用人需求改善、裁員規模下降，勞動市場仍
維持穩健，供需情勢持續朝平衡方向發展。 
⚫ 9 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差 1,050 億美元，較上月減少 0.7%。出口成
長 1.9%，高於進口增幅 1.2%，主因資本財及黃豆等農產品出口增加；
進口則受資本財及消費財需求帶動。累計 1 至 9 月貿易逆差較去年同期
縮減 12.2%，顯示對外貿易失衡情況有所改善。

事件

115.11.9 
一、 美國—就業及企業活動持續改善，期中選舉牽動財政與科技監管政策 
2026 年 10 月美國非農就業人數增加 16.5 萬人，明顯高於上月修正後之 6.0
萬人，亦高於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 4.8 萬人之水準，企業聘僱動能有所回
升；失業率則由 4.2%降至 4.1%，持續維持於 3 月以來 4.1%至 4.3%之區間，勞動
市場整體仍具穩定性。產業別方面，新增就業主要由專業及商業服務業增加 7.0
萬人、醫療保健業增加 4.2 萬人及運輸倉儲業增加 2.5 萬人所帶動，製造業亦增
加 1.9 萬人，其餘產業就業變動幅度相對有限。薪資方面，私人部門平均時薪升
至 38.0 美元，月增 0.4%、年增 3.1%，增幅較前月略有加快；平均每週工時則維
持 34.4 小時不變。此外，8 月及 9 月新增就業人數合計上修 8.4 萬人，反映上兩
月勞動市場表現較初估時有所改善。整體而言，10 月美國新增就業明顯回升、失
業率小幅下降，加上前兩個月就業數據獲得上修，勞動市場有所改善，整體仍具
韌性；惟新增就業主要集中於部分服務業，且因經濟因素從事兼職工作的人數增
加，反映勞動市場仍存在就業不足及產業表現不均的情況。 
2026 年 9 月美國職位空缺數(Job Openings)由上月修正後之 719.0 萬個增加
33.3 萬個至 752.3 萬個，職缺率由 4.3%升至 4.5%，企業徵才需求略有回溫；聘僱
人數由 527.1 萬人增加至 540.2 萬人，聘僱率由 3.3%升至 3.4%。總離職人數由
517.5 萬人增加至 531.9 萬人，離職率維持於 3.3%。就離職結構觀察，主動離職
(Quits)人數由 304.4 萬人增加至 336.5 萬人，主動離職率由 1.9%升至 2.1%，勞工
轉職意願有所回升；裁員與解僱(Layoffs and Discharges)人數則由 176.8 萬人減少
至 164.1 萬人，裁員率由 1.1%降至 1.0%，企業人力調整壓力仍屬溫和。產業別方
面，職位空缺增加主要來自州及地方政府(不含教育)、專業與商業服務業、製造
業及營建業；聘僱人數則以住宿與餐飲服務業增幅較為明顯；醫療保健與社會救
助業裁員人數則有所下降。整體而言，9 月美國職缺數與聘僱人數同步增加，主
動離職意願回升且裁員規模下降，顯示企業用人需求有所改善，勞動市場仍維持
穩健，惟供需關係持續朝較為平衡方向發展。 
2026 年 10 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)由上月的
54.5 升至 55.5，增加 1.0 點，連續第 10 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，製造業景

氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由 55.3 升至 55.5，接單情況持續改善；生產
指數由 56.7 攀升至 59.0，製造活動進一步增溫；就業指數雖由 52.7 微降至 52.4，
但仍維持擴張。供應商交貨指數由 59.0 微升至 59.1，反映交貨時間持續延長；庫
存指數由 48.6 升至 51.4，顯示企業備貨意願回升。此外，價格指數由 77.9 降至
75.2，投入成本壓力雖略有緩和但仍處高檔；未完成訂單指數雖由 56.4 降至 54.6，
但仍在擴張區間。另一方面，新出口訂單指數由 50.9 升至 51.2，進口指數由 51.0
升至 52.9，均維持擴張區間，顯示外需及企業採購需求仍具支撐。整體而言，10
月美國製造業活動持續穩健擴張，新訂單、生產及庫存表現改善，顯示需求與生
產動能仍具韌性；惟投入成本仍處相對高檔，加上關稅政策、地緣政治情勢及供
應鏈變數猶存，後續對企業成本控制及製造業景氣發展之影響仍值得持續關注。 
2026 年 10 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)為 54.5，較上月
54.9 下降 0.4 個百分點，惟已連續第 28 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近
12 個月平均值 54.3，美國服務業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 56.5
降至 53.8，新訂單指數由 59.8 降至 55.9，顯示需求與營運動能較前月放緩，但仍
維持擴張；就業指數由 50.1 升至 51.4，連續第 2 個月處於擴張區間，反映企業用
人需求仍具韌性。供應商交貨指數由 53.2 升至 56.7，交貨時間進一步延長；價格
指數由 74.0 降至 73.0，雖較前月回落，但已連續 23 個月高於 60，企業成本壓力
仍高。庫存指數由 57.8 降至 55.4，未完成訂單指數則由 56.6 升至 58.0，創 2022
年 7 月以來最高，顯示需求累積情形持續增加。此外，新出口訂單指數由 46.9 大
幅升至 55.2，重返擴張區間，進口指數亦由 52.9 升至 54.2，顯示外部需求及採購
活動回溫。ISM 指出，關稅及燃料成本仍是企業最關注的供應鏈議題，許多企業
尚未完全將增加成本轉嫁給客戶，未來仍須持續關注關稅、能源價格及成本壓力
對服務業景氣與通膨之影響；惟值得注意的是，10 月所有主要指標均位於擴張區
間，為 2026 年 2 月以來首見，顯示整體服務業景氣基礎仍具支撐。 
2026 年 9 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 1,050 億美元，較上月減少 7 億美元
(-0.7%)。當月出口 3,215 億美元，較上月增加 58 億美元(+1.9%)；進口 4,266 億美
元，增加 52 億美元(+1.2%)，出口增幅高於進口，使貿易逆差略為改善。其中，
商品貿易逆差縮減至 1,359 億美元(-0.5%)，服務貿易順差略降至 309 億美元(-
0.1%)。出口成長主要受資本財出口增加 38 億美元帶動，其中電腦、電腦周邊設
備及民用航空器表現較佳；食品、飼料及飲料出口亦增加 19 億美元，以黃豆增幅

### Page 7

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

6

最為顯著。進口方面，資本財進口增加 116 億美元，消費財進口增加 24 億美元，
惟工業用品及原材料進口減少 37 億美元。累計 2026 年 1 至 9 月，美國商品及服
務貿易逆差為 6,623 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少-12.2%；同期出口成長 11.6%，
進口增加 6.2%，顯示對外貿易失衡情況較去年同期有所改善。 
2026 年美國期中選舉呈現反執政黨效應，民主黨重掌國會主導權，截至 2026
年 11 月 8 日，美國期中選舉開票結果顯示，民主黨已確定取得國會參、眾兩院
控制權。其中，參議院民主黨陣營取得 54 席，共和黨 44 席；眾議院民主黨取得
254 席，共和黨 162 席。此外，全美州長席次分布方面，民主黨掌握 28 席，共和
黨 20 席。由於部分選區仍在計票或尚未確認選舉結果，最終席次仍待確定。民主
黨並於全國眾議院普選票(Popular Vote)獲得較高得票率，且在德州、堪薩斯州、
北卡羅來納州、俄亥俄州及南卡羅來納州等關鍵州選戰取得突破，反映選民對高
物價、能源價格上漲、中東衝突及部分政策方向的不滿情緒升高。此次選舉終結
共和黨自 2024 年總統大選以來同時掌控白宮與國會兩院的局面，美國再度形成
「分裂政府」(Divided Government)格局。未來川普政府推動重要法案、預算案、
人事任命及稅制改革等政策，將面臨更大的國會制衡與協商壓力，民主黨亦可能
加強對行政部門的監督與調查。就經濟層面而言，市場預期大規模減稅、財政擴
張及部分貿易政策推動速度可能放緩，有助於降低財政赤字進一步擴大的風險；
惟兩黨對立加深，亦可能提高預算協商、債務上限及政府融資等議題的不確定性。 
美國民主黨於 2026 年 11 月期中選舉取得國會參、眾兩院控制權後，未來人
工智慧監管政策走向受到關注。其中，由無黨籍參議員桑德斯（Bernie Sanders）
及民主黨眾議員卡薩爾（Greg Casar）於 9 月分別提出的《2026 年禁止人工超級
智慧法案》（Ban Artificial Superintelligence Act of 2026），主張禁止開發、部署、
持有及資助具備超越人類認知能力或可能威脅人類安全的人工超級智慧（ASI），
並設立內閣層級的聯邦「人工智慧部」（Department of Artificial Intelligence），
統籌先進 AI 系統的安全審查及監管。此外，法案規劃在監管機構完成籌設並建
立相關安全規範前，暫停訓練運算量達 10²⁵次以上的先進 AI 系統進行訓練、修改
及微調，並限制未發布系統的部署。截至 11 月 8 日，該法案仍處於國會委員會
審議階段，尚未正式立法。隨著民主黨將於 2027 年 1 月取得國會主導權，後續
法案審議進展及其對美國 AI 技術發展、企業投資與國際競爭的影響，值得持續
關注。

二、歐元區—通膨壓力持續增強 
 歐元區 10 月 HICP 通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 4%，核心通膨(不含食品及能源)
則持平為 2.5%。該月食品及能源通膨皆持續上揚，反映國際原油價格居高不下，
並透過肥料、物流成本上漲，間接推升食品價格。另外，服務通膨上升 0.1 個百
分點至 3.3%，部分反映交通費用上漲的影響，惟服務通膨仍是低於今年上半年的
平均水準。展望未來，近期布蘭特原油雖回落至 100 美元內，但美伊談判陷入僵
局，仍預期年底前布蘭特原油位於每桶 90 美元以上，將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後
續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等成本管道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，
進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
 其他經濟數據包含：(1)歐元區 9 月失業率上升 0.1 個百分點為 6.5%，失業人
數增加 12.2 萬人，主要是法國失業人數增加較多所致，係因大規模抗議活動的影
響；(2)歐元區 9 月零售額月減 0.7%、第三季季減 0.5%，物價上漲帶來的負面影
響逐步顯現，預期第四季零售額將延續此一疲態。 
三、日本—薪資成長動能增強，春鬥效應持續擴散 
 9 月薪資年增 4.6%，創 1997 年以來最大增幅，除受夏季獎金大增 32.6%支撐
外，經常性薪資亦年增 3.7%，顯示今年春鬥的加薪成果正逐步由大型企業擴散至
中小企業。隨企業獲利保持強勁，加上日本最大工會聯合組織（Rengo）已表態明
年將爭取更高加薪幅度，預期薪資成長動能仍可延續，有助支撐民間消費，但同
時也可能推升服務價格，增加通膨持續性。 
四、中國—關注 11 中旬川習會發展 
10 月官方製造業 PMI 下降 0.8 點至 49.3，主因十一長假前需求提前釋放，以
及外部環境不確定性升高所致。新訂單、生產指數分別降至 49.4、49.8，新出口
訂單亦降至 48.7。此外，生產及出廠價格指數分別降至 56.8 及 51.7，反映成本與
售價漲幅已有收斂跡象；惟就業指數則持平於 48.4，顯示就業仍顯疲弱。不過，
RatingDog 製造業 PMI 略增 0.1 點至 52.1，反映 RatingDog 調查以中小型出口企
業為主，在全球 AI 相關需求的支持下，仍能保持穩定的擴張表現；惟 RatingDog
製造業產出物價指數連續兩個月下調，投入物價指數則持續上揚，反映競爭環境
激烈，迫使廠商犧牲利潤以降價求售。

官方非製造業 PMI 下降 0.5 點至 49.7，其中建築業商務活動指數由 50.3 降至
48.0，服務業商務活動指數則小幅下降至 50，顯示十一長假消費動能並不特別活
躍；非製造業就業指數雖微升至 46.0，但仍遠低於榮枯線，不利於後續內需復甦。
另外，RatingDog 服務業指數下降 0.8 點至 50.8，亦反映需求增速減弱，使得
RatingDog 綜合產出指數為 52，較 9 月的 52.4 回落，為近三個月最慢，製造業產
出增快為能抵銷服務業擴張放緩。 
展望未來，中國製造業仍面臨外需不確定、內需復甦偏弱及價格競爭激烈等，
惟全球 AI 產業需求可望擴張，加上人行上調外匯存款準備率，有助緩和人民幣
升值對出口競爭力的壓力，接有助於繼續支持出口需求。此外，若 11 月 APEC 期
間川習會釋出改善中美經貿關係訊號，將有助提振企業信心及出口前景，惟後續
仍須觀察全球需求及地緣政治發展。 
五、印度及東南亞—馬來西亞央行按兵不動；印尼、越南出口亮眼；泰國通膨壓
力上升 
(1) 印尼：9 月出口額達 275.9 億美元，創四年新高、年增 11.87%，非油氣出口較
去年成長 12.39%，以動植物油脂成長 89.04%為最，礦物燃料及電氣機械及設
備分別成長 12%、24.37%，抵銷石油和天然氣出口下滑 1%的影響，對美國、
中國出口分別成長 23.21%、11.82%。9 月進口額達 242.3 億美元、年增 18.41%，
增幅創 6 個月新低，其中成品油進口成長 37%，非石油和天然氣進口額年增
17.59%，以電氣機械設備(25.9%)及塑膠製品(16.3%)增幅較大，自中國進口額
大增 30.23%、自東協和歐盟進口分別成長 20.98%、18.21%。總計 9 月，貿易
順差為 33.6 億美元，2026 年前 9 個月，貿易順差 106.1 億美元，出口和進口
分別成長 5.58%和 19.67%。 
(2) 泰國：10 月消費者物價年增率(CPI)從 9 月的 2.82%飆升至 3.9%，3 年多來首
次突破泰國央行 1%-3%的目標區間，主要受油價上漲和洪水導致食品價格上
漲的影響，泰國商務部預計 2026 年整體通膨率平均在 1.8%-2.2%之間，但由
於基期較低，物價壓力可能在年底前持續走高。雖然通膨壓力顯著增強，但官
方評估此波物價飆漲應屬暫時現象。 
(3) 馬來西亞：馬來西亞央行(BNM)11 月會議將隔夜政策利率（OPR）維持在 2.75%
不變，為連續第八次維持利率不變，符合市場預期。政策聲明強化對經濟成長

的樂觀評估，表示在強勁的出口表現和持續的內需支撐下，2026 年全年經濟

通膨方面，央行指出地緣政治發展、大宗商品價格上漲及不利天氣狀況將對通

(4) 越南: 10 月出口額年增 51.4%，達 646.7 億美元的歷史新高，其中電子產品出
口年增 102.6%為主要成長動力，機械設備出口年增 49.8%居次。同時，10 月
進口額達 573.6 億美元、年增幅略放緩至 43.1%，其中，電子產品進口成長
84.5%，能源進口則因油價上揚而大幅增加 74.1%。總計 10 月，貿易順差為
73.1 億美元，連續第二個月轉為順差；2026 年前 10 月，貿易逆差小幅收斂至
120.7 億美元，出口額成長放緩至 27.5%、進口額成長率為 37.4%。10 月 CPI
年通膨率小幅上升至 5.09%，創 5 月以來最高，其中，以交通運輸費用上漲
15.57%為最，酒精飲料和菸草（4.40%）、住房和公用事業（6.50%）及醫療保
健（1.75%）價格漲幅均有所加快，由於通膨已連三月突破央行目標區間，將
加大升息的壓力。 
六、台灣—通膨漲幅收斂，尚無輸入性通膨疑慮 
10 月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.33%，較 9 月的 2.74%明顯回落，顯示整
體通膨壓力已有降溫跡象，但仍近半年皆高於 2%。10 月物價漲幅收斂，主要受
蔬菜價格轉跌、電價漲幅縮小，以及部分商品與服務價格漲勢趨緩等因素影響，
惟外食費年增 3.01%，已連續 5 個月超過 3%，民眾對物價上漲的感受仍較明顯。
另，10 月以新台幣計價的進口物價年增 33.32%，創近 50 年最大漲幅，主要受國
際原油及 AI 需求推升電子零組件價格所致，惟相關電子零組件多供出口使用，
對國內消費端影響有限，加以政府採取油價平穩措施，因此主計總處認為目前並
無輸入性通膨問題。展望未來，隨中秋節比較基期因素消退，主計總處預估 11 月
CPI 年增率仍將高於 2%，且漲幅可能較 10 月擴大。

2026 年 10 月 30 日

調査レポート

2026 年 7～9 月期の GDP（1 次速報）予測

～ほぼゼロ成長も、緩やかな景気回復は継続～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田 隼平

2026 年 11 月 16 日に内閣府が公表する 7～9 月期の実質 GDP（1 次速報値）は、前期比＋0.0%（前期比年
率換算＋0.1%）と予測する。設備投資や政府消費の増加に支えられ、かろうじて 4 四半期連続のプラス成長を
維持したものの、個人消費や輸出が前期の一時的な押し上げ要因の剥落もあって減少したことで、ほぼゼロ成長
にとどまったと見込まれる。なお、輸入が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直したことで外需寄与度は大きめのマイナスと
なるものの、公的在庫のプラス寄与により、おおむね相殺されたと考えられる。 
また、名目 GDP は、前期比＋0.9%（年率換算＋3.8%）となったと見込まれる。コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進
む中で、GDP デフレーターは前年比＋2.7%（季節調整済前期比では＋0.9%）と高めの伸びが続いた。 
今回、7～9 月期の実質 GDP はほぼゼロ成長にとどまる見込みだが、イラン情勢の悪化という逆風を踏まえれ
ば、国内景気は底堅さを維持している。先行きについては、輸入コストの高止まりに伴う価格転嫁の進展が景気
の重しとなる可能性はあるものの、良好な雇用・所得環境や企業業績の改善を背景に内需の回復基調が維持さ
れることなどから、国内景気は緩やかな回復を続けると期待される。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度 
（出所）内閣府「四半期別 GDP 速報」により作成

### Page 2

2 / 2

需要項目別の動向は以下の通りである。

① 実質個人消費（前期比－0.1%） 
 良好な所得環境が維持される中、消費者マインドも持ち直しへ向かったものの、前期に耐久財
が自動車やエアコンを中心に一時的な要因によって増加した反動が生じたとみられる。価格転
嫁の進展による物価上昇、地震や台風といった自然災害の発生も重しとなった可能性がある。 
② 実質住宅投資（前期比－0.3%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数が横ばいとなる中、建築コストが上昇していることもあり、住
宅投資は小幅ながら減少が続いたとみられる。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比＋0.9%） 
 企業の投資意欲は省力化やデジタル需要などに支えられて堅調であり、設備投資は 3 四半期
ぶりに増加したと見込まれる。機械設備投資の供給側統計である資本財総供給（除く輸送機械）
も 7～9 月期は前期比＋5.3%と増加した。 
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度－0.0%ポイント） 
 前期に供給制約懸念の高まりを受けて、メーカーが製品在庫などの積み増しを進めた反動もあ
り、7～9 月期はマイナス寄与になったと考えられる。 
 なお、公的在庫については、政府による代替調達の進展により石油備蓄の減少ペースが大きく鈍
化したことから、前期比寄与度＋0.5%ポイントとプラス寄与になったとみられる。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費支出（前期比＋0.3%） 
 高齢化が進む中、医療費・介護費を中心に底堅く推移したと予測する。 
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比－2.7%） 
 建築・土木コストの上昇が続いている中、7～8 月の建設総合統計（出来高ベース：公共）は弱
めの動きとなったことから、実質公共投資は 2 四半期連続で減少したと見込まれる。 
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度－0.5%ポイント、実質輸出：前期比－0.5%、実質輸入：同＋1.7%） 
 輸出は前期比－0.5%と減少したと予測する。財は堅調な米国向けや中東向けの持ち直しを受
けて増加した一方、サービスは前期に研究開発サービスが一時的な要因により上振れた反動
で減少したとみられる。 
 輸入は前期比＋1.7%と増加したと予測する。特に財が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直した。ただ
し、サービスについては減少した可能性がある。 
 結果、輸出から輸入を差し引いた外需の寄与度は－0.5%ポイントとマイナスになったと見込ま
れる。

2317 鴻海精密工業(FOXCONN)

報告日期：2026/11/13 研究員：曾智楷(Kevin Tseng) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 營收 3.02 兆，YoY +46% ，毛利率 6.02%，EPS 5.51
元。單季營收、營業利益、稅後淨利均創下歷年單季新高。 
 受惠於 AI 營運持續成長及 ICT 產品進入傳統出貨旺季，公司
預期 4Q26 整體營收季對季與年對年均將呈現強勁成長。AI 伺
服器部分，由於新一代機櫃市佔率較上一代提升，預期第四季
AI 機櫃出貨量將維持高雙位數季增。 
 訂單能見度轉佳，公司上修 2026 年全年四大產品展望，對 2027
年全年營運持審慎樂觀看法，預計整體營收維持雙位數成長，
AI 機櫃則預估明年出貨維持高雙位數成長。

一. 3Q26 營收、獲利續創單季新高，EPS 5.51 元 
鴻海 3Q26 營收 3.02 兆，QoQ +19% ，YoY +46% ，續創歷史新
高，主要受惠於 AI 伺服器呈現強勁成長，其中，第三季 ASIC AI
伺服器出貨量年增 1.5 倍，高於 GPU 約 70%的成長率，帶動整體
AI 機櫃達成高雙位數成長目標，且新一代 AI 機櫃已開始出貨。就
各產品別來看，3Q26 雲端網路產品佔整體營收的 54%；消費智
能產品佔 29%；電腦終端產品佔 12%；元件及其他產品佔 5%，
而獲利方面，3Q26 營業毛利 1815.1 億，QoQ +17%，YoY +39%，
毛利率 6.02%，QoQ -0.1ppts，YoY -0.33ppts，營業利益 1153.5
億，QoQ +22% ，YoY+64%，營利率 3.83%，QoQ +0.07ppts ，
YoY +0.4ppts，3Q26 業外淨利 33.6 億，稅後淨利 775.2 億，QoQ 
+29%，YoY +34%，單季稅後 EPS 5.51 元，創歷年單季新高。

二. AI 機櫃放量、ICT 產品進入旺季，4Q26 營運有望持
續成長 
公司公告 10 月營收 1 兆 3053 億，MoM +12.7%，YoY +45.7%，
今年累計營收 8.97 兆，YoY +40.4% 。展望 4Q26，受惠於 AI 營
運持續成長及 ICT 產品進入傳統出貨旺季，公司預期整體營收季
對季與年對年均將呈現強勁成長。其中，AI 伺服器部分，由於新
一代機櫃市佔率較上一代提升，預期第四季 AI 機櫃出貨量將維持
高雙位數季增。而傳統 ICT 產品，消費智能產品在旺季需求與 ASP
提升下，預期將呈現強勁表現；電腦終端產品則受限於記憶體等
零組件供應吃緊影響，預期將略微季減。預估公司 4Q26 營收 3.59
兆，QoQ +18.7%，YoY +37.9%，毛利率 5.8%，營利率 3.5%，
稅後淨利 811 億元，單季稅後 EPS 5.77 元。

三. 預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 19.08/23.59 元 
展望 2026 年，由於目前訂單能見度較 8 月更佳，公司上調 2026
年全年四大產品展望，整體營運維持強勁成長。預估 2026 全年營
收 11.26 兆，YoY +39%，毛利率 6.0%，營利率 3.7%，稅後淨利
2685 億，全年稅後 EPS 19.08 元。展望 2027 年，公司對明年全
年營運持審慎樂觀看法，預計維持雙位數成長，其中 AI 仍為最主
要的成長動能。受惠於全球算力需求與基礎設施投資，全球主要
CSP 明年持續擴大 AI 資本支出，預期公司雲網產品佔比將在未來
3 到 5 年，從目前的五成以上進一步提高至 60%以上。而 AI 機櫃
部分，預期 2027 年 AI 機櫃出貨維持高雙位數成長，且客戶除了
大型 CSP 客戶之外，明年將加入更多 NeoCloud 客戶，市佔率將
進一步提升。此外，隨 AI 推論需求增加帶動 ASIC 伺服器快速成
長，其高度客製化需求亦有利公司發揮零組件垂直整合優勢以支
撐獲利表現。消費智能產品由於主要客戶產品以高階為主，受記
憶體等供應鏈短缺影響相對有限，再加上新產品開發與規格升級
需求，預計 2027 年整體表現相對穩定。而電腦終端產品，由於記
憶體供應緊張與零組件價格上漲，可能推升終端售價並影響消費
者換機意願，對明年 PC 市場看法較為審慎。預估 2027 全年營收
14.9 兆，YoY +33%，毛 利率 5.4%，營利率 3.3%，稅後淨利 3320
億，全年稅後 EPS 23.59 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 330 元 
考量到 CSP 持續上修 2027 年資本支出展望，AI 伺服器維持強勁
需求，隨著新一代 AI 機櫃推出，有望進一步拓展公司在 AI 伺服
器領域市佔率，帶動公司營收、獲利持續成長。目前公司股價交
易於 2027 年 EPS 約 10~11 倍本益比，評價仍有提升空間，給予
投資建議為買進，目標價 330 元(14x 2027 EPS)。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
鴻海精密工業(FOXCONN)為全球最大 eCMMS(電子化-零元件、
模組機光電垂直整合服務)廠，在電子代工服務領域(EMS)排名全
球第一，市占率超過四成。鴻海近年積極投入「電動車、數位健
康、機器人」三大新興產業以及「人工智慧、半導體、新世代通
訊技術」三項新技術領域，以「3+3」結合作為集團重要的長期發
展策略。在 EMS 板塊，鴻海主要競爭對手為和碩聯合科技、廣達
電腦、緯創資通、仁寶電腦、英業達及立訊精密等。

二.產業趨勢概況 
1. 明年 CSP Capex 預估破 1.5 兆美元，AI 伺服器產值年增逾 9 成 
根據集邦科技(TrendForce)最新研究，受惠於 Google、Amazon、
Meta、Microsoft、Oracle 及中國四大科技巨頭等全球前九大雲端
服務供應商(CSP)擴大投資 AI 基礎設施，預估 2026 年九大 CSP
資本支出總額將逾 9,570 億美元，YoY +106%，2027 年更因伺服
器整機漲價、零組件缺貨漲價及供應商要求預付款等因素，有望
突破 1.5 兆美元，YoY +60%，雙雙優於預期。在超大型 CSP、Tier-2
資料中心與主權 AI 等需求帶動下，預估 2026 年 AI 伺服器產值約
4,790 億美元，占整體伺服器產值近 75%；2027 年隨輝達 GB300、
VR200 與超微 Helios 等整櫃式方案成為主流，加上記憶體等關鍵
零組件短缺推升單價，預計整體伺服器產值將首度跨越 1 兆美元，
其中 AI 伺服器產值將躍升至近 9,200 億美元 YoY +90%，占比進
一步提高至 81%左右。

2. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型(LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28%更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中，Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展，2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

## Document 129: 261113_daiwa_ Lotes

Source PDF: all report pdf/261113_daiwa_ Lotes.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Lotes’s 3Q26 results, released on 13 November.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. Ongoing gross margin dilution due 
to rising raw material costs and less benefits from the AI server trend has 
been, in our view, the primary drag on Lotes’s share price over this year. 
We believe this trend may reverse now as investors are worried about the 
potential AI capex cuts impacting the overall Taiwan supply chain’s revenue 
outlook in 2028, whereas Lotes only has 3-5% of exposure currently. Also, 
management expects gross margin pressure to start easing with the new 
product mass production (eg, SOCAMM) starting from 4Q26. Second, 
Lotes’s regular server-related products may see less chance for inventory 
correction as its products are not in shortage now. Lastly, its desktop 
business has suffered more than the overall industry’s headwinds (ie, unit 
decline 10-15% YoY) as its desktop products had to downgrade from DDR5 
to DDR4/3 due to component shortages, which further negatively impacted 
profitability. We believe these three headwinds will gradually transform into 
tailwinds once the AI capex starts to slow down. However, if the AI server 
capex trend remains as is, we could also see some upside for the 
company. Lotes is still eager to develop a GPU socket for the Rubin Ultra 
generation, which we believe could become a new growth driver. Besides, 
quick disconnect (QD) and NPO sockets might also become growth drivers. 
While QD only accounts for low single digit of total revenue from 3Q26, we 
expect it to have a chance to increase to c.10% of total revenue in 2027E.

3Q26 profitability missed expectations. Lotes’s 3Q26 earnings came in 
at TWD1.92bn (EPS: TWD17.1), which was 11%/14% lower than our/street 
estimates. Despite revenue largely in-line with expectations, gross margin 
decreased by 0.6pp QoQ to 44.8%, mainly from rising copper and gold 
prices; unfavourable product mix (rising high-speed cable and connect and 
QD) may be another reason. However, operating margin rose 0.9pp QoQ to 
22.9% mainly on the lack of one-off employee stock bonus expense (as 
done in 2Q26) but opex was still higher than the company’s guidance 
(<TWD2.0bn) due to salary increase for its Chinese branches.

What we recommend: We lower 2026-27E EPS by 2-7% to factor in 3Q26 
results and lower profitability recovery assumption but maintain 2028E 
EPS. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call but lower our 12M TP to TWD2,480 (from 
TWD2,850), based on a lower PER of 22x (from 28x; high end of its past-5-
year range of 10-28x), on our 1-year forward EPS, given the weaker market 
sentiment. Key risk: lower-than-expected adoption of server CPU platforms.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E revenues are 5-10% higher than the 
Bloomberg consensus, likely due to our more bullish view on QDs.

13 November 2026

3Q26 review: bottomed-out gross margin

➢ 3Q GM slightly declined on rising material cost but likely bottomed out 
➢ Rising QD, SOCAMM, NPO sockets to be key growth drivers in 2027 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD2,480

Source: Daiwa forecasts

Company profile 
Founded in 1986 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2007, Lotes is one of the world’s leading 
connector vendors specialising in CPU socket/ILM and connectors, such as DDR, PCIe, SATA, and 
Type-C, with most of its production capacities at its Guangzhou, Zhongshan and Suzhou plants. Its 
superior manufacturing capability and strong client relationships are the key differentiators that make 
Lotes stand out from its competitors. Since the PC market became saturated from 2015, Lotes has 
focused on penetrating non-PC connector businesses, such as connectors for servers, automotive and 
industrial, and new types of cables (eg, Thunderbolt cable).

### Page 5

5

Lotes (3533 TT): 13 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 13 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Lotes’ board consists of 7 directors, including 3 independent directors. The total number of
directors is slightly lower than other listed companies in our coverage. Lotes held 8 board
meetings in 2025 and the actual attendance rate was 95%.
Although the Chairman and CEO are not the same person, they are, in fact, brothers and the co-
founders of Lotes. It gives us a sense that Lotes is still a family enterprise, which is likely a
negative for the board and executive management. That said, the background of independent
directors is diversified with expertise in technology and management. We see it as a positive for
the board's quality.
Capital management 2
Lotes declared a DPS of TWD41.5 in 2025, representing a payout ratio of 50%. Being a leading
connector vendor for applications such as desktops, notebooks and servers, Lotes grew rapidly
along with its key clients (Intel and AMD) on strong technical capabilities and higher growth rate
from the server market. The solid revenue CAGR in the past decade required higher working
capital and continuous capex to expand its capacity, which could also be the culprit for its slightly
lower payout ratio compared with other electronic component makers. Having said that, we think
Lotes’ higher revenue growth should partially offset the drawback of a lower payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, there were no disclosed related-party sales, while purchases from related parties
amounted to TWD4.4m, representing 0.03% of total cost of sales. We see limited risk from
related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
Lotes established its RBA management system in 2010 and requires its suppliers to meet the
standards of no child labour, no forced labour, treating employees with respect and dignity, and
caring for labourers' safety and health. Lotes hosts a yearly supplier conference to promote the
RBA guidelines to its suppliers. In 2025, 187 suppliers participated in the annual supplier
conference. Lotes also requires its suppliers to sign the RBA-related commitment letters. For
better supply chain management, Lotes conducts yearly audits and regular business reviews
and holds the yearly supplier conference as measures. In 2025, Lotes conducted written
evaluations of 110 suppliers and on-site audits of 61 suppliers. In addition, all 76 newly added
suppliers passed its environmental and social responsibility evaluations. Lotes has required new
suppliers to sign the "Supplier Social and Environmental Responsibility Commitment Letter"
since 2010 to extend social responsibility concepts and requirements to the supply chain.
For "green" purchase management, Lotes has adopted the IECQ QC 080000 hazardous
substance process management system. Lotes asks its suppliers to follow the RoHS, HSF, and
REACH guidelines to avoid using hazardous materials. Lotes also conducts conflict minerals due
diligence and requires suppliers to sign non-conflict mineral declarations and complete surveys
tracing mineral sources to smelters. Overall, Lotes believes it has constructed a comprehensive
system covering system certification (e.g., ISO 9001), product design, supply chain
management, and production techniques to ensure that the entire manufacturing process, from
raw materials to end products, follows green principles. All products from Lotes are now 100%
compliant with RoHS standards.
S Data security 2
Lotes has established a team to implement data security regulations, planning and maintenance.
Moreover, it monitors vulnerabilities in its information systems each year through regular
vulnerability scanning, access control reviews and information security audits. The company
reported no losses resulting from information security incidents in 2025.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

## Document 130: 261113_daiwa_Gigabyte

Source PDF: all report pdf/261113_daiwa_Gigabyte.pdf
Report source: line報告備份

### Page 1

Summary: Gigabyte released its 3Q26 results after market hours on 13 November, with 
net income of TWD8.57bn (EPS: TWD12.07) beating expectations, while revenue of 
TWD154.7bn (+7% QoQ, +94% YoY) was largely in line despite robust server demand. 
Gross margin expanded 4.4pp QoQ to 14.0% (vs. our 9.6% estimate). Management cited 
factors such as higher value-added products and better cost control, though we believe 
VGA card price hikes were the key driver. Despite strong gross margin expansion and 
operating leverage, higher profit-sharing bonuses limited OPM expansion to 2.7pp QoQ to 
8.3%, still above our/consensus estimates of 5.8%/5.9%. We expect Gigabyte to benefit 
from solid AI server demand from neoclouds as they increase power budgets and capex, 
with neoclouds and enterprises becoming key Nvidia server buyers from Vera Rubin in 
2027. Nebius reaffirmed its 5GW contracted-power and 0.8-1GW connected-power end-
2026 targets, with >1GW annual deployments from 2027; CoreWeave raised its 2026 
capex guidance to USD37-39bn, with USD132.5bn in backlog. As neoclouds serve 
sovereign and enterprise customers lacking in-house hardware capabilities, we expect 
TAM growth for server brands including Dell, Gigabyte, Lenovo and Asustek. We reaffirm 
our AI server revenue forecasts of TWD412bn in 2026E and TWD565bn in 2027E. We 
expect the AI server mix to dilute the GM to 8.9% in 2027E and 8.4% in 2028E. We 
reiterate our Buy (1) rating with a 12M TP of TWD490, based on 15x the 1-year-forward 
diluted EPS; key downside risk is Nvidia’s business model change.

What’s the impact: 
Below are our read-throughs from Gigabyte’s 3Q26 results (released on 13 November).

• 3Q26 results first-take: profitability beat expectations. Gigabyte’s net income of 
TWD8.57bn (EPS: TWD12.07) beat our/Bloomberg consensus estimates by 32%/29%, 
respectively. Revenue (TWD154.7bn; up 7% QoQ and 94% YoY) was largely in-line with 
estimates, despite robust server demand. Nevertheless, the gross margin expanded by 
4.4pp QoQ to 14.0% in 3Q26 (exceeding our estimate of 9.6%). Management attributed 
the strength to an improved product mix, higher value-added products, strategic 
procurement, and better cost control, but we believe price hikes in VGA cards to be the 
key factor. Despite strong gross margin expansion and operating leverage, higher profit-
sharing bonus expenses caused the operating margin to only rise by 2.7pp QoQ to 8.3% 
(still beating our/street’s estimates of 5.8%/5.9%).

• Business outlook. We believe Gigabyte will ride on consistently solid AI server demand 
from the neoclouds, as key vendors continue to raise their power budget and capex. 
This aligns with our view that the neoclouds and enterprise will gradually become key

buyers of Nvidia AI servers starting from the Vera Rubin generation in 2027. Nebius 
(NBIS US, NR) reaffirmed its end-2026 contracted-power target of 5GW (up from >4GW 
currently contracted) and connected-power target of 0.8-1GW, with plans to deploy 
>1GW annually starting in 2027. Meanwhile, Coreweave (CRWV US, NR) raised its 
2026 capex guidance to USD37-39bn (from USD35-39bn) with an order backlog of 
USD132.5bn (vs. USD3.7bn revenue in 3Q26). We believe the neoclouds will become a 
key computing source for sovereign, enterprise and other corporate customers that 
cannot design and maintain the hardware themselves. As such, we expect the TAM for 
server brands like Dell (DELL US, USD371.17, Outperform [2], covered by Louis 
Miscioscia), Gigabyte, Lenovo (992 HK, NR) and Asustek (2357 TT, NR) to rise.

While management expects continued revenue growth and improving operating 
leverage in 2027, we reaffirm our AI server revenue assumptions for Gigabyte of 
TWD412bn in 2026E and TWD565bn in 2027E (vs. TWD180bn in 2025). We also 
believe the rising contribution from AI servers will be structurally margin-dilutive, despite 
potential benefits from higher value-added solutions, strategic procurement, and 
economies of scale. We therefore forecast gross margin to gradually normalise to 8.9% 
in 2027E and 8.4% in 2028E (vs. 14.0% in 3Q26).

What we recommend: 
We have a Buy (1) rating on Gigabyte, with a 12M TP of TWD490, based on a target PER 
of 15x, applied to our 1-year-forward diluted EPS. Key downside risk: earnings impact from 
Nvidia’s business model change.

M
Update
TSMC  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
AI demand and key strategy: TSMC remains confident in the AI mega trend.
Technology roadmap and capex capacity planning are the most critical decisions for
company's long-term development.
US power constraint: Regarding the US power infrastructure shortage concerns,
TSMC has its own internal assessment process and disciplines, and combining with
customers' feedback, there is no change in the demand forecast.

Capacity and US expansion: TSMC is still aiming to more than double its 2nm
capacity by next year, given strong demand from AI and smartphone. The company
also continues to evaluate the next US fab site, and there was a discussion with
Tesla for TeraFab cooperation in parallel.
GPU and ASIC customers: TSMC will continue support major GPU customers, as it
does with other customers' needs. Both GPU and ASIC are TSMC's customers, and
TSMC have been balancing customers' needs.

Pricing and margin: TSMC's pricing reflects its value and cost inflation. Although
semiconductor and foundry remain a cyclical industry, TSMC hopes its margin will
be consistent and sustainable through the cycle.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

雙鴻(3324)：3Q26 財報不如預期，營運逐季向上 
建議：維持買進

一、雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商  雙鴻成立於 1998 年 8 月，為國內前二大 NB 散熱模組廠，過去主要生產散熱
器、散熱片、散熱模組、散熱導管、風扇等，現在已轉型為提供客戶完整散熱解
決方案供應商。主要應用於筆記型電腦、桌上型電腦、智慧型手機及伺服器等領
域。3Q26 產品應用領域 Server 81%、PC 14%、VGA/Gaming 4%、及 Others(手
機及車用) 1%。另外 3Q26 水冷業務比重 62%(1H26 約為 55~60%)。

二、3Q26 毛利率 25.65%，低於
預期，3Q26 EPS 11.59 元 
 雙鴻 3Q26 EPS 11.59 元，低於台新預估的 14.13 元，獲利達成率 82%。本業
來看，3Q26 營收 97.87 億元，+12.5%QOQ，+64.3%YOY，低於市場預期之
+15~20%QOQ，七月營收有遞延認列，實際拉貨力道未如預期顯著，3Q26 毛
利率 25.65%，較 2Q26 再衰退 2.77 個百分點，亦低於台新預估的 30%，主要
是報廢品打消，預期 4Q26 毛利率回升，3Q26 EPS 11.59 元。雙鴻 10 月營收
33.72 億元，+17%MOM，+45%YOY，獲利達成率 31%，符合預期，預期 11 月
營收及 12 月營收將優於 10 月，預估 4Q26 營收 110.37 億元，+12.8%QOQ，
+45.6%YOY，預估 4Q26 毛利率 28%，預估 4Q26 EPS 14.79 元。

三、營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷
兩塊業務均樂觀 
 2025 年雙鴻水冷營收比重 35%，1Q26 提升至 55%，3Q26 提升至 62%，預期
2027 年將提升至 80%，帶動公司業績大幅增長。氣冷部分，2024 年月營收 2-
2.5 億元，2025 年 4～4.5 億元，2026 年平均 6～7 億元，氣冷水冷均樂觀，以
AWS TR3 來看，氣冷及水冷比重至少是 70~80%對比 20~30%，TR4 將為 100%
全水冷，水冷大趨勢不變，VR 9 月起量，VR 營收逐季增，TR3 9 月起量 Compute 
tray 及 Switch tray 及 Rack manifolds 都有相關產品出貨，預計 12 月單月 ASIC
營收比重有機會來到 15~25%，ASIC 水冷產值高於GPU 客戶，其中 Mata>AWS，
2027 年 ASIC 增長可期，公司預期 2027 年 ASIC 營收將超過 GPU。2026 年可
視為 CDU 元年，2027 年營收跳增可期。台新預估 4Q26 營收 110.37 億元，

公司拜訪快報 
+10~15%QOQ，2026 年營收+60~70%YOY，4Q26 毛利率 28%，2027 年營收
及毛利率維持正向看法，雙面水冷板及水冷板+底座新設計助於平均 ASP 及毛
利率提升。針對市場擔憂 AI 放緩疑慮，公司認為 AI 趨勢不會變，即便成長率有
所調整仍正面看待，提升算力方向不會停下，雙鴻 2026 年級 2027 年亦將維持
高資本支出。

四、維持買進理由  3Q26 毛利率仍不如預期，故下修未來各季毛利率，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務樂觀，
水冷擴張趨勢明確，故維持買進，預估 2026 年 EPS 49.26 元(原估 55.74 元)，
預估 2027 年 EPS 76.62 元(原估 90.36 元)，目標價 1200 元。

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

## Document 302: Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms

Source PDF: all report pdf/Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Blue Wave: Democrats 
sweep the US midterms  
Summary 
Event: The Democrats gained control of the House of 
Representatives and the Senate at the US midterm elections 
that took place this week.  
Our View: With the US government now no longer under 
unified control of the Republicans, gridlock has returned to 
Washington. We believe investors should not overreact to 
these results and should stay invested. 
In equities, investors should continue to diversify their 
equity exposure into more defensive sectors. As we 
transition to mid-cycle for the equity markets, we expect 
stocks to continue to deliver positive returns in the 8-10% 
range per annum, a downshift from the torrid pace of gains in 
the earlier part of the cycle.  
In bonds, income remains attractive given recent spread 
widening. We favour non-hyperscaler investment grade 
corporate bonds at the three to seven-year part of the 
curve. We continue to expect the US dollar to remain range-
bound in the near term, as we expect two more rate hikes 
from the Fed, but as this mini rate hike cycle ends in the 
second quarter of 2027, the USD index (DXY) is likely to 
depreciate towards 98.5 as markets start to anticipate a Fed 
rate cut in the second half of next year.  
Oil is likely to rebound in the short-term on heightened 
geopolitical risk, but as supply chains adapt to the current 
situation and Congress exercises oversight on defense 
spending, WTI prices are likely to drift towards our 12-month 
target of USD 80 per barrel.

Democrats made solid gains across the Senate, House 
of Representatives and State Governors 
Results reported as at 7:45PM Singapore Time

Source: The Wall Street Journal, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

Major implications of Democrat control of 
Congress   
1) Fiscal expansionary policies may slow. Democrats are 
now in position to block any new tax cuts or spending plans 
from the Trump administration. At the same time, any fiscal 
policies proposed by Democrats may also face Trump’s veto. 
Hence the fiscal trajectory of the US will likely remain stuck 
over the next two years. As we get into the second half of 
2027, the US debt ceiling will once again loom, and gridlock 
could potentially bring back government shutdowns. 
However, as they have always done in the past, compromise 
will ultimately prevail, in our opinion.  
2) Trump remains in control of foreign policy. With the 
midterms now out of the way, could Trump decide to re-start 
combat operations with Iran? We believe there is a risk that 
US and Israel – now that both countries’ have concluded 
important elections – will once again be tempted to restart 
combat operations to force an end to the conflict. However, 
the duration of any renewed hostilities may be limited by 
Congress next year as the Democrats will likely withhold 
funding for further military operations. In the near-term, oil 
prices may experience renewed volatility before stabilising at 
a lower level next year. The introduction of new tariffs on 
trading partners may prove harder with Democrat oversight. 
3) AI data centre moratoriums could grow, though model 
development regulatory changes would be harder to 
pass. While the headlines have focused on the House and 
Senate results, one important development has also 
occurred at the gubernatorial level. Notably, Democrats have 
won the governor races in Texas and Georgia, two big data 
centre states. As data centre build out permits are largely 
decided at the local state level, this will have important 
ramifications for build out plans next year. However, any 
Democratic proposals to regulate AI model developments 
would need to be signed off by President Trump, who 
continues to wield veto power on any such proposals.   
Asset class strategy 
Equities: Remain invested and continue to diversify.  
While investors initially overreacted, President Trump’s 
relatively conciliatory response has provided some welcome 
relief, helping markets recover strongly from their pre-market 
lows. Hence, we recommend investors avoid 
overreacting, remain invested and continue to diversify 
their equity exposure across sectors and regions.  
A few themes stand out from the early pre-market reaction to 
the mid-term elections. On the positive side, defensive stocks 
continue to fare well, while themes favoured by Democrats 
are also outperforming. Clean energy is a notable 
beneficiary, with solar stocks among the biggest winners

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation cycle 
is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle transition, 
where equity returns moderate but remain positive

Standard Chartered Bank

today. Affordability-related themes are also rallying, including 
companies that could benefit from potential tariff rollbacks. 
Medicaid-related stocks are also moving higher on 
expectations that potential policy changes could provide a 
tailwind for the healthcare sector. 
On the negative side, semiconductor weakness continues 
amid concerns around potential data centre moratoriums. 
Our AI capex estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around 
data centre spending, investors should remain selective. 
After downgrading semiconductors last month, we have 
maintained a conservative stance on the industry, with a 
lower recommended allocation compared to September. 
While we believe the mid-term election outcome is not a 
complete surprise to markets, particularly given pre-election 
polling and the recent weakness in both the S&P 500 and 
Nasdaq, the overnight correction highlights how complacent 
investors had become, with the VIX initially surging more than 
10% in pre-market trading, although down from intra-day 
highs after President Trump’s comments. 
As we have highlighted in our recent notes on the transition 
to mid-cycle, this phase is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid-term election outcome 
reinforces the trend. The experience of 2015-2017, 
highlighted in the second table, offers a useful historical 
parallel. Despite the knee-jerk reaction, investors should not 
overreact based on one day’s moves. While we had trimmed 
equities to a core allocation last week, we continue to expect 
equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per annum 
during the mid-cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady underlying earnings growth in both 2027 
and 2028. 
So, what should investors do to navigate the next few months 
before the newly elected representatives are sworn in and 
take control of the House and Senate? As we have been 
highlighting in recent notes, equity markets should still deliver 
positive returns, albeit at a more moderate pace. Hence, we 
recommend staying invested but diversification remains key 
to navigating the next phase of the cycle.  
FX: DXY near-term support at 100.7, 12-month target at 
98.5. A Democratic sweep potentially removes the legislative 
runway for further tariff escalation and for fiscal expansion, 
the two structural pillars of US exceptionalism that have 
anchored dollar demand in 2026. Reduced fiscal impulse 
compresses the growth and yield differential that has kept the 
US dollar well-bid all year. Additionally, potential Democratic 
restrictions on AI and data centre investment could weigh on 
US equity outperformance, which is likely to act as a 
secondary dollar-negative channel.  
However, despite historical readings supporting a near-term 
DXY headwind from a Democratic sweep, the magnitude will

likely be modest and short-lived unless compounded by a 
weaker than expected non-farm payrolls report on Friday. We 
see the Fed narrative, not the election, as the dominant 
medium-term driver. Near-term we see USD index (DXY) 
support at 100.7, and retain our 12-month target at 98.5.  
Fixed Income: Still attractive all-in yields; prefer 
investment grade corporates at three-to-seven year 
tenors to earn income and manage interest rate risk. The 
US Treasury bond market reaction appears to reflect a 
moderation in expectations of future economic growth after 
the election results. Yields at the intermediate end of the 
curve dropped the most, while the 30-year US yield 
moderated more modestly. We continue to expect two more 
Fed rate hikes between now and H1 2027 as still sticky 
inflation and the robust economy support further tightening.  
In the past few weeks, the US Treasury yield curve has 
broadly shifted downwards, after our opportunistic call to go 
long 10-year Treasuries at the end of September. We are of 
the view the ultra-long end downshift in yields is overdone 
(given the still challenging US fiscal trajectory) and would 
recommend investors who had earlier taken long duration 
exposures at the 20 to 30-year parts of the curve reduce their 
positions now. We continue to believe the 3 to 7-year part of 
the curve offers the most optimal balance of income and 
interest rate risk, particularly via investment corporate bonds. 
Within corporate credit, we expect a potentially slower pace 
of AI data centre buildouts could marginally improve market 
technicals, as hyperscaler and tech sector bond issuance 
may slow (and reduce “crowding out” of other bond 
issuances). Conversely, from a credit perspective, we believe 
this dynamic may also make the data centre focused deals 
less viable, and would be cautious on some of this sector’s 
lower quality names. Meanwhile, less AI capex dependent 
issuers continue to offer solid credit profiles amidst the still 
strong economy. High yield corporate spreads have widened 
in recent weeks but dispersion within the complex is wide, 
with BBs and Bs credit spreads staying relatively resilient, 
while CCCs have widened to distressed levels. For income-
focused investors, we would recommend staying within the 
crossover range of between BBB-BB.  
Commodities: As noted in the opening section, we believe 
there is a higher near-term risk of a restart of combat 
operations in Iran given the conclusion of the midterm 
elections. Given that, we believe there is a risk that oil 
prices could see a short-term spike, despite recent 
diplomatic overtures from Iran. However, for 2027 we 
continue to expect oil to stabilise at lower levels, as we 
think the Mideast situation should de-escalate given 
Democratic oversight of the defense budget, and the likely re-
configuration of oil supply chains. On the margin, we are also 
closely monitoring regulatory developments in the US shale

We expect oil prices to drift towards USD 80 a barrel as 
oil supply chains adapt, and Congress restrains the US 
defense budget

On gold, we view the recent change in control in the

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +9.57 điểm tương ứng +0.55%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Tập đoàn công nghiệp, Tiện ích Điện và Bất động sản là các nhóm ngành có mức tăng 
rõ rệt nhất trong tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với 
giá trị -810 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, VCB và STB; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -13 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -1,161 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -12,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -2,033 
hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,103 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua tuần hồi phục nhẹ với biến động tăng/giảm đan xen, thanh khoản 
ở mức thấp và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài tiếp tục đẩy mạnh bán ròng. Hai phiên giữa tuần 
chứng kiến sự hồi phục mạnh mẽ của thị trường, phản ứng trước thông tin NHNN có động 
thái hỗ trợ thanh khoản hệ thống ngân hàng để hạ lãi suất thông qua nghiệp vụ hoán đổi 
ngoại tệ, và mua vào dự trữ ngoại hối. Dù vậy, áp lực bán nhanh chóng quay trở lại ở 2 
phiên cuối tuần khi nhà đầu tư hoài nghi về triển vọng hạ lãi suất trong ngắn hạn, trong 
khi TTCK toàn cầu cũng có diễn biến kém khả quan trước đà hồi phục của giá dầu, số liệu 
CPI Mỹ cao hơn kỳ vọng, và diễn biến tiêu cực ở nhóm cổ phiếu AI.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành thêm một mẫu nến spinning trên khung tuần, hàm ý tương 
quan cung cầu đang tạm thời cân bằng; trong khi diễn biến tăng nhẹ về điểm số nhưng đi 
kèm sự sụt giảm của thanh khoản cho thấy tín hiệu hồi phục mang tính phân hóa rõ nét. 
Trên khung ngày, nhịp bật tăng tích cực của chỉ số sau khi chạm đường biên dưới của mẫu 
hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn đang cho dấu hiệu chững lại tại vùng kháng cự gần 
quanh 1.750, phản ánh lực cầu mặc dù được củng cố khá tốt ở vùng giá thấp nhưng thiếu 
sự đồng thuận khớp lên các vùng giá cao. Diễn biến giằng co có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy 
nhiên khi xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn đang nghiêng về chiều tăng, chỉ số được kỳ vọng sẽ tiếp 
tục hướng lên, thử thách lại các vùng cản phía trên. 
(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 320: Cliff Notes_ risks mount (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ risks mount (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: risks mount
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
10:31 November 20 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October, employment falling 43.5k following
September’s 36.4k decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the year have now slowed
to 1.1%yr, materially below current growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the

unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9% despite the participation rate falling
another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

### Page 2

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer momentum in hiring, now we are seeing
evidence of a material increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have remained in
employment to date, but persistent job shedding raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend
in the unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most
certainly before the late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3
increase was skewed towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay outcomes directly
tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements
meanwhile, the most responsive to current and expected labour market conditions, accounted for their
smallest share of a Q3 wage increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour cost

growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident in housing. The Westpac Housing
Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005. Interestingly,
there remains considerable latent demand: almost 13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over
the next year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak sentiment, high interest rates and

expectations of further price declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These factors also
pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for inflation remained the critical theme. The
October FOMC minutes highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs and energy
prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several [members] judg[ing] that price pressures were
increasingly extending beyond industries directly affected by data center construction”. Participants

"assessed that medium- and longer-term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-target inflation is a threat to the status
quo, and perceived risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke throughout the week
also kept the focus on price risks, and called for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again
proposed a 50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at the December and January

meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales
gave further justification for this view this week, the control group surprising to the upside with a 0.6%
gain in October after a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said, housing is under
considerable pressure, pending home sales falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back

at 2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent weakness in this sector will, in time, have
implications for both consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international development of the week. Again, it provided
a call for pro-active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr
and the decline in fixed asset investment broadened outside property, with capital expenditure now

down 8.1% across the economy year-to-date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth; though, even here, momentum softened
in October, annual growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not only activity that

### Page 3

Related articles
remains under pressure, but also wealth, with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective, stimulus needs to affect change in both activity
and wealth and do so in a sustainable manner.

STRONG RESILIENCE TO EXTERNAL SHOCKS
, 
SOLID 
MACROECONOMIC FUNDAMENTALS 
◼ 
 Over the past 
 two years
 , India has demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of external shocks, while its 
macroeconomic fundamentals have remained solid.  
◼ 
 In FY2025/2026  
(ended in March 2026)
 , India was hit by a sharp rise in US tariffs. However, thanks to supportive fiscal 
and monetary policies, 
 real GDP  
grew by 7.8% in FY2026, placing India among the fastest
 -
 growing Asian economies.  
◼ 
 For the current fiscal year, the Indian economy is facing rising energy prices 
 (
due to 
 the conflict in the Middle East and 
increased food prices 
 because 
 of 
an 
unfavourable monsoon
 ) 
as well as 
 monetary tightening. Nevertheless, despite these 
new challenges, real GDP growth is expected to exceed 7%.  
◼ 
 India is currently in a stronger position than it was in 2022 to cope with the new energy shock. The fiscal leeway to support  
the economy has increased with the decline in the fiscal deficit, and inflation is more contained.  
◼ 
 Although 
 rising  
energy prices have put pressure on external accounts, FX reserves 
 remain  
sufficient to cover the country’s 
short
 -
 term external financing needs. 
 Yet, 
downward pressures on the rupee have been 
 significant 
 and 
could persist in the 
short term
 . 
In 
H1 
2026, India’s external accounts withstood the rise in energy prices fairly well
 , notably  
thanks to the sharp increase 
in workers’ remittances. 
 T
he 
widening of the trade deficit was primarily due to the substantial increase in machinery and 
equipment imports 
 while t
 he oil trade deficit increased 
 only 
moderately, as the rise in energy prices was offset by a decline 
in import volumes. 
 However, 
in H2 2026, the oil trade deficit is expected to widen significantly.  
Additionally, large portfolio outflows have exacerbated downward pressure on the rupee, necessitating interventions by 
the central bank in foreign exchange markets and the adoption of regulatory measures to support the currency. Although 
these measures hav
 e led to a rebound in the rupee (at least temporarily), inflationary pressures, volatile portfolio 
investments, and low net foreign direct investments are 
 expected to continue 
 to put pressure on the currency.  
◼ 
 D
ownward pressures on the rupee 
 and 
 rising bond yields will reduce the government's 
 already limited 
 fiscal capacity to 
increase public investment and 
 support economic development. However, this will not undermine its ability to refinance 
its debt, given its safe structure.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector is sufficiently robust to cope with the expected rise in credit risks.

STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH DESPITE 
STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINTS 
◼ 
 In October 2026, the IMF revised upward 
 India’s  
medium
 -
 term growth 
 forecast 
 from 6.5% to 6.8%  
per year
 . Nonetheless, 
to achieve such strong growth performance, 
 India will have to adapt to the challenges posed by artificial intelligence  
(AI)
, 
while its infrastructure and education system are not prepared for it.  
◼ 
 Moreover, India’s development remains highly uneven. 
 The middle class is still modest
 . This is explained by a large 
informal economy, heavy concentration of employment in low value
 -
 added sectors such as agriculture, low participation 
rates among women and youth, and limited industrial development. All this, in turn, reflects India’s weak i
 ntegration into 
global value chains.  
◼ 
 India faces two key structural challenges: i
 ) adapting the services sector to AI and ii) accelerating industrial development. 
Even with progress in sectors such as smartphones and pharmaceuticals, India’s industrial base remains too small to 
absorb its vast labour force. 
 Labo
u
r market 
 rigidities  
and the mismatch between training and business needs are holding 
back both the creation of quality jobs and industrial development.  
◼ 
 Low net foreign direct investment is a 
 major  
 obstacle. While 
 India attracts 
 new 
 FDI, retention is weak
 : 
 despite 
improvements in logistics, governance issues and regulatory barriers have 
 led 
 disinvestments to triple over the past five 
years.  
◼ 
 India 
 may fail in completing its 
 transition to a higher
 -
 value, more industrialised and inclusive economy if reforms do not 
accelerate 
 significantly
 . Structural change
 s 
could lag behind pressures from its rapidly growing population and 
 fast 
global 
technological advances
 .

INDIA
’S COMPLEX POSITION 
IN INTERNATIONAL TRADE 
Low 
attractiveness  
due to unfavourable business environment and limited integration in global value chains  
◼ 
 Despite very strong growth, India's attractiveness is low relative to the size of its economy. The stock of foreign direct 
investment stood at only 15% of GDP in Q2 2026 compared to 52% of GDP in Vietnam and around 67% of GDP in Thailand 
and Malaysia.  
◼ 
 This lack of appeal can be attributed to 
 India’s still unfavo
 u
rable 
 business environment and 
 its 
 limited integration into 
global value chains. India’s global market share remain
 s 
 low (1.9% of total 
 world 
 exports  
 of goods
 )
, 
 especially in 
comparison with that of China (16.1% of total exports).  
◼ 
 India has not benefited from the AI boom
 , 
since its exports of AI
 -
 related goods remain modest (0.5% of its total exports)
 . 
However, 
 it has benefited from the “China +1” strategy adopted by 
 international 
 companies seeking to diversify their 
production sources. Smartphone exports by India have increased 6.3
 -
 fold over the past five years and accounted for almost 
6% of global exports in 2025 compared to less than 1% five year
 s 
earlier.  
To 
increase its a
 ttractiveness and develop its industrial sector, India has 
 multiplied F
 ree 
T
rade 
 A
greements  
◼ 
 Over the past 18 months, 
 India  
has signed a growing number of free trade agreements 
 – 
with the European Free Trade 
Association in October 2025, the United Kingdom in July 2025, Oman in December 2025, and the European Union in January 
2026
. This 
 should help to develop the industrial base and demonstrates the Modi government's commitment to 
accelerating India's integration into global trade.  
The sectors most likely to benefit from the recent trade agreement with the EU are electronics and semiconductors, 
pharmaceuticals and chemical
 s
, textile and renewable energy technologies. But expanding trade agreements without deep 
domestic improvements in the business environment, governance, education, and infrastructure will be ineffective. India 
will continue to struggle with investment reten
 tion. 
◼ 
 The US
 -
 India bilateral trade agreement has not yet been concluded. The two sides reached a framework for an interim 
Agreement in February 2026, under which the US would apply an 18% reciprocal tariff on Indian goods, with further 
reductions subject to the 
 successful conclusion of the agreement. As of early October, negotiations 
 are 
stalled. Key sticking 
points include market access, particularly 
 in 
India’s sensitive agricultural sector.

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE

Non transfer risk   
India’s external accounts are solid with a low external debt, moderate current account deficit and large FX 
reserves. 
 India’s net debtor position reached only 5.9% of GDP in Q2 2026. In the short term, external accounts 
are expected to deteriorate 
 given 
the rise in energy prices while downward pressures on the rupee should remain 
high.  
◼ 
 External accounts have remained solid 
 despite recent adverse shocks
 . 
 In 2025, external accounts remained solid 
despite the significant increase in US tariffs. In 
 H1 
 2026, they held up well against rising energy prices. Although the 
current account deficit is expected to widen for the current fiscal year as a whole, it should remain contained, and foreign 
exchange reserves should be more than sufficient to cover the co
 untry’s short
 -
 term financing needs. The 
 major  
causes 
for concern are the 
 structurally 
 low level of foreign direct investment (although it is rising) and the increased reliance on 
portfolio investment, which exposes the currency to greater volatility.  
◼ 
 Structural vulnerability to energy price shock
 . 
India is structurally vulnerable to an energy price shock as its 
 oil and gas  
trade deficit is relatively high (2.8% of GDP in 2025). However, in H1 2026, the current account withstood fairly well the 
rise in energy prices especially thanks to the sharp increase in workers’ remittances  
(+0.7 pp to 3.7% of GDP)
 . The trade 
deficit widened significantly but principally because of the strong increase in machinery and equipment imports rather

 is projected

 Currency risk
 :

 Its

risk is limited given 
 its 
sound

is

◼ 
 In FY2026 (ended in March 2026), public finances continued to improve. 
 The central government’s fiscal deficit 
narrowed to 4.4% of GDP from 5% of GDP the previous year. 
 However, t
 his reduction stemmed primarily from a significant 
rise in dividends from the central bank. 
 G
overnment revenues remained low at  
9.6% of GDP, with tax revenues stagnant 
at 
7.6% of GDP. Besides, debt

3.6% of GDP, equivalent to  
37.6% of total government 
revenue
s

The fiscal position is stronger than during the 2022 energy crisis, with the general government deficit estimated 
at 
7.8% of GDP  
in FY2026, compared to  
9.5% 
in FY2021/2022.  
◼ 
 For FY2027, the initial budget targets a slight reduction in the central 
 government 
 deficit, to 4.3% of GDP. This goal may 
however 
 prove challenging due to rising subsidies amid the energy shock.  
To cushion households from the shock, the government initially 
 kept 
petrol prices 
 unchanged
 . Refining firms absorbed the 
financial burden, partially offset by lower excise duties on petroleum products. Only after regional elections in mid
 -
 May 
did the government allow a modest increase in pump prices to reflect higher international 
 prices
 . 
The government has 
also increased subsidies  
to offset the impact of rising fertiliser prices on farmers.  
Revenue losses  
—
driven by

duties, reduced dividends from state
 -
 owned enterprises, and the economic slowdown
 — 
could reach  
0.5% of GDP
 , while 
the cost of 
 fertiliser subsidies 
 could 
 rise from  
0.4% to 0.8% of GDP.  
To reduce the risk of fiscal slippage, the government 
has: i) raised taxes on fuel exports, ii) capped household diesel purchases; and iii) planned cuts to certain expenditures.  
◼ 
 While the primary deficit reduction  
 target may prove difficult to achieve,

 is 
another source of severe concern

 . This is expected to

Systemic credit risk  
The banking sector has strengthened over the past five years
 , 
as illustrated by the improvement in 
 asset  
quality 
and capital buffers. The main sources of credit risks are rising household debt, the rise in energy prices, the 
unfavourable monsoon and monetary tightening.  
◼ 
 The quality of assets has improved significantly. 
 In Q1 2026, the banking sector’s non
 -
 performing loan (NPL) ratio fell 
to a multi
 -
 decade low of 1.8%. For public banks  
(
54.4% of total loans
 )
, the NPL ratio declined to 2.1%.  
◼ 
 Capital buffers are strong. 
 While 
loan loss provisions remained modest at 75.6% of NPLs, capital buffers stayed strong. 
The Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR) rose to 17.7% in Q1 2026, up from 17.4% a year earlier. From April 2027, Indian banks 
will adopt a new Expected Credit Loss (ECL) framewo
 rk, requiring earlier provisioning for potential loan losses. In the 
short term, higher provisioning requirements could push up lending rates and tighten credit conditions 
 while 
 triggering 
capital raising or targeted government recapitalisation. 
 I
n the medium term, the reform could strengthen banking  
sector 
resilience through earlier recognition of credit risks and more robust loss buffers.  
◼ 
 Banks’ 
 liquidity 
 is 
 comfortable
 . 
It deteriorated slightly in H1 2026 because credit growth outpaced deposit growth
 ; t
he 
liquidity coverage ratio decli
 ned to 
a still solid 
 124% 
and 
the loan
 -
 to
-
 deposit ratio increased to 83% in Q2 2026 from 79.1% 
a year earlier. RBI interventions to support the rupee 
 also 
 drained liquidity
 . Moreover
 , the deposit mix shifted from low
 -
cost current and savings accounts (CASA) to higher
 -
 cost term deposits, pressuring net interest margins. However, since 
June 2026, liquidity has improved significantly due to RBI measures to attract foreign
 -
 currency non
 -
 re
sident (FCNR) 
deposits.  
◼ 
 Profitability remains satisfactory. 
 It 
 has declined since the start of the year, primarily due to narrowing net interest 
margins, but remains satisfactory. In Q2 2026, 
 the 
 return on assets (ROA) and return on equity (ROE) stood at 1.6% and 
13.0%, respectively (IMF data).  
◼ 
 P
rivate
 -
 sector  
 debt 
 is moderate
 , 
 but its increase 
 needs to be monitored.  
 It stood 
 at 105.1% of GDP in Q1 2026 (BIS  
data
), compared to 100.1% five years earlier
 , which is 
 lower than in other emerging Asian economies.  
Non
-
 financial corporat
 e 
debt was 56% of GDP in Q1 2026. Listed firms’ financial positions continued to strengthen amid 
surging profits, improving their ability to service debt. In Q2 2026, the 
 average 
 interest coverage ratio (ICR) rose to 7.4, up 
from 6.1 a year earlier. However, the share of companies with an ICR below 1 (insufficient earnings to cover interest costs) 
increased to 18.5%. Stress remain
 s 
 concentrated in micro
 -
 enterprises, agriculture, and energy
 -
 intensive sectors (
 e.g.
, 
power, steel, cement, aviation), which are 
 the 
most exposed to oil
 -

Household leverage requires monitoring. Although still moderate, household debt increased to 49.1% of GDP in Q1 2026, 
up from 42.7% five years earlier, driven largely by non
 -
 housing retail loans (58.4% of total borrowing).  
◼ 
 Monetary 
 policy 
 tightening. 
 Due to rising  
inflation (+5.5% year
 -
 on
-
 year in September) and downward pressure on the 
rupee, the RBI raised its policy rate by 25 basis points to 5.5% in October 2026 under a "calibrated tightening" stance.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector should remain resilient  
 despite rising risks in the short term. 
 Due to rising energy prices, a poor 
monsoon, and monetary tightening, asset quality is expected to deteriorate, particularly for low
 -
 income borrowers, micro, 
and small enterprises. 
 E
arly warning signs are emerging
 , with some 
 borrowers starting to miss payments. The stress is 
concentrated in unsecured retail loans  
(
which account for over half of fresh retail slippages
 )
. 
This could lead to new 
 NPLs 
over the coming quarters, especially as higher rates and inflation squeeze repayment capacity. Additionally, in the near 
term, capital inflows may remain weak, 
 and 
 liquidity and funding pressures could intensify.

射頻元件 
IET-KY (4971 TT)

調降評等 
中立(Neutral) 
展望 2027年營運正向，雙位數成長可期  
⚫ 3Q26毛利率低於預期，磷化銦產品比重下滑 
⚫ 2027年基板供應將改善，磷化銦產品仍為重要成長動能 
⚫ 砷化鎵光電產品持續開發，有望成為新營運動能 
3Q26財報低於預期：公司於 2026年 11月 12日舉行第三季法說會，
3Q26 營收 3.19 億元(QoQ+24.64%，YoY+26.84%)、毛利率 38.72% 
(QoQ+4.72ppts，YoY+7.05ppts)、營業利益率 18.16 %(QoQ+9.73ppts，
YoY+4.75ppts)、稅後淨利 5,933 萬元(QoQ+214%，YoY+98%)、單季
1.26元 EPS。3Q26營收及毛利率低於我們預期，主要因磷化銦基板供
應仍受限制，德系基板供應商產能爬坡進展不如預期，而主要日系供
應商供貨份額未有太大改變，使高毛利磷化銦產品營收佔比自前季
48.7%下滑至 33.4%，且砷化鎵產品營收占比因相關射頻及國防訂單顯
著上升，影響產品組合。3Q26營業費用 0.65億元大約符合我們預期，
因公司持續投注研發費用於專業機台進行較高階產品的研發，費用較
往年明顯提升。 
磷化銦磊晶片仍是 2027年主要成長動能：2027年成長動能仍來自磷
化銦磊晶片，雖 3Q26供應狀況未如我們先前預期，公司認為磷化銦
基板供應於 4Q26已有些微放緩跡象，展望 2027年供應限制有望進一
步改善，預期毛利率將回升至四成以上水準。2027年公司展望營收成
長約 10%至 25%，新興 APD及 HBT磊晶產品也預計於 2027年量產，
而除磷化銦外，4Q26起砷化鎵及銻化鎵國防與商用訂單顯著成長，其
中銻化鎵紅外磊晶片已加速訂單需求，預期 2027 年訂單持續擴大，
並增加產能。磷化銦基板供應未如先前所預期顯著改善以及公司明年
展望狀況，我們下修 2027年營收及獲利預估。 
砷化鎵光電產品靜待多點開花：為降低對傳統磷化銦基板供應的依
賴，公司積極開發 QD Laser、變晶型 PIN等新產品切入光通訊市場，
其中，QD Laser 已受到客戶初步良好反饋，進行送樣當中。 200G 
VCSEL方面也持續進行研發，並已與客戶配合 NRE。 
受股數稀釋獲利及市場影響下調評價：公司與光聖進行股份交換使流
通在外股數增加致流通在外股數上升，我們預估 2026、2027年 EPS 
分別為 5.08、7.66元，此外，考量近期市場評價有較大幅度修正，較
前次報告下調本益比，我們基於 2027預估 EPS x 50倍本益比，目標
價為 383元，調降至中立之投資評等建議。風險因子：基板供應短缺。

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

US Big Tech earnings, mid-term elections and major central bank policy meetings are the near-term market drivers 
Consensus S&P500 index Q3 earnings estimates by sector   Chance of Democrats winning control of US House and Senate*

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *Polymarket probability

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary: We retain our near-term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid-term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3-7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15-year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US? Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities among all scenarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer-term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fallible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bonds (CoCos1) 
and US 10-year government bond ideas: We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10-year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:  Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share-price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monetisation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence. We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight, while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long-term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While France’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro 
area bank equities offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5-3.0% q/q, led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased, despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs. Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited, supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist
